b) 2. Kh4. Prevent the pawn from advancing? But the pawn is already at h5. Black Kxg4. Result: Black has a pawn, White has no pawn. Black wins.
c) 2. Kf3/Kf4. Black can take the pawn 2... Kxg4. Result: Black has a pawn, White has no pawn. Black wins.

If 1... g6 (Kg6), White might play 2. Kg3 (wait it's already there) or something else.
The position is very symmetrical.
The goal in these endgames is often to force a draw or win the opposite colored pawn.

Actually, if I play 1... h5, I'm forcing White to either give up the pawn or accept a draw.
If White plays gxh5, it's a draw.
If White doesn't take h5, I take g4 (Kxg4), then I have a pawn and White doesn't. I can potentially win.
